\paragraph{The grid in one picture (2026-10-05, the paper's summary).} Fig.~\ref{fig:gain_heatmap}
(\code{gain\_heatmap.py}, the $8$k grid) gives each probe--pretraining pair the mean over $D$ of
$\log_{10}(L_{\rm scratch}/L_{\rm fine\text{-}tune})$, the leader rule of Fig.~\ref{fig:rung_focus}. Of the $840$ cells
$698$ ($83\%$) fine-tune below scratch, and $106$ of the $120$ pairs gain on average. The geometric mean of the gain over
all cells is $1.70$ from rung $0$, $2.1$--$2.4$ from rungs $1$--$6$ and $3.2$--$3.4$ from rungs $7$--$9$; from rungs $7$--$9$ it is
$2.5\times$ at $D\le10^2$ and $3.7$--$4.2\times$ at $D\ge10^{2.5}$. The rung adding a probe's structure beats the rung below on
nine of twelve probes (not $u\bar u\to Zg$, $ee\to b\bar b$ at one loop, $ee\to Z\gamma$), and the best rung is that one
or a later one on nine of twelve (not $ee\to W^+W^-$, best from rung $3$, and the two one-loop probes, best from rungs $5$
and $7$). $ee\to Z\gamma$ is the one probe no pretraining helps ($0.5$--$1.2\times$). The scratch curve of the $W$-pole
arm's probe $u\bar d\to e^+\nu_e$ levels off at $1$--$2\times10^{-4}$ from $D=10^{3.5}$: every top trial there has its best
checkpoint at the last of the $8$k steps and its lr inside the window, so it is the horizon, not the search.
\begin{figure}[H]
\centering
\includegraphics{../analysis/transfer/figs/gain_heatmap.pdf}
\caption{The fine-tune's gain over scratch, $\langle\log_{10}(L_{\rm scratch}/L_{\rm fine\text{-}tune})\rangle_D$, per probe and
pretraining (rung $0$: $ee\to u\bar u$, $64$k steps), the $8$k grid; outlined, the first rung with the probe's structure.}
\label{fig:gain_heatmap}
\end{figure}

\paragraph{Distance from the pretraining (2026-10-05).} Each pretraining's zero-shot loss on each probe
(\code{tools/zero\_shot\_ft.py}: the parent's weights scored on the probe's validation split before any fine-tune step,
in the probe's standardized units, where a constant prediction of the mean scores $\approx1$), all $10\times12$ pairs,
against the gain of Fig.~\ref{fig:gain_heatmap} (Fig.~\ref{fig:zero_shot_gain}, \code{zero\_shot\_gain.py}). A pretraining
that already predicts a probe gains more on it: the Spearman correlation of the log zero-shot loss with the gain is
$-0.41$ over all pairs ($p=3\times10^{-6}$) and negative within ten of the twelve probes ($-0.26$ to $-0.81$; $ee\to\nu_e\bar\nu_e$
$+0.12$, $ee\to t\bar t$ $-0.05$). Closeness is not needed for a gain, however: $ee\to u\bar u$ scores $0.99$--$1.13$ zero-shot
on seven probes and $1.9$ and $2.4$ on $ee\to W^+W^-$ and $ee\to\nu_e\bar\nu_e$, no better than a constant, and still
fine-tunes below scratch at a geometric mean of $1.7\times$. Only $ee\to d\bar d$ ($0.28$), $ee\to t\bar t$ ($0.66$) and
$ud\to ud$ ($0.79$) start below the constant from it.
\begin{figure}[H]
\centering
\includegraphics{../analysis/transfer/figs/zero_shot_gain_legend.pdf}\\
\includegraphics{../analysis/transfer/figs/zero_shot_gain.pdf}
\caption{Zero-shot loss of each pretraining on each probe (standardized $\log|\mathcal M|^2$, before any fine-tune step)
against the fine-tune's gain over scratch of Fig.~\ref{fig:gain_heatmap}; one point per pretraining and probe, $8$k grid.}
\label{fig:zero_shot_gain}
\end{figure}

\paragraph{A $32$k horizon at $D=10^3$: no clear gain from one run.} One run per probe at its cell's best $8$k HPs, $32$k
steps (\code{job\_transfer\_calib.sh}, Jean Zay, 2026-10-04): against the $8$k cell, $ee\to t\bar t$ gains $4.0\times$,
$ud\to ud$ $1.5\times$, $ee\to Z\gamma$ $1.2\times$; $ee\to d\bar d$, the one-loop probes, $u\bar u\to Zgg$, $Zggg$ and
$ee\to\nu_e\bar\nu_e$ move by $\le1.2\times$ or rise ($0.3$--$1.0\times$); several best checkpoints sit early
($4800$--$12480$ of $32$k steps: over-training at $D=10^3$), and $u\bar u\to gg$ diverged at step $28224$ (its best before
that not read). One run against a best-of-eight search is biased toward the $8$k value, so the $8$k cells stand at
$D=10^3$.

\paragraph{The star arms' four new probes, and the arms.} The new probes (Fig.~\ref{fig:rung_grid_new}) fine-tuned from rung
$1$ and from the $64$k $ee\to u\bar u$: on $ee\to d\bar d$ at one loop above $500$\,GeV both are $30$--$250\times$ below scratch at
$D\le10^{2.5}$ and $1.5$--$4\times$ above (the QCD-virtual target at $\mu=\sqrt s$ is close to the Born, which both pretrained
on); on $ee\to t\bar t$ at one loop near threshold both are $2.5$--$20\times$ \emph{worse} than scratch at $D\le10^{2.5}$, and rung $1$
is $1.6$--$3.6\times$ better from $10^3$ up. Each arm against rung $1$ on its own probe (Fig.~\ref{fig:star_arms},
\code{star\_arms.py}; which arms test their structure: hand-off): soft and collinear emission ($ee\to d\bar dg$) $3$--$8\times$
lower at every $D$; initial-state collinear ($u\bar u\to Zg$) $2$--$7\times$; the $t\bar t$ threshold $2$--$4\times$ from $10^3$ up,
still above scratch at small $D$; the dead cone ($ee\to t\bar tg$) level, $2\times$ worse at $10^4$; the resonance and Sudakov
controls level, the Sudakov one up to $20\times$ worse at $D=10$. On $ee\to d\bar d$ every arm stays with rung $1$, within
$4\times$ at $10^4$: added structure costs the near probe little.

and rung $1$ at the same two chosen points per cell. (g) $ee\to d\bar d$ fine-tuned from every arm; rung $1$ solid for its

\paragraph{The star arms' design, and the two that tested nothing.} Six arms (the user approved them, 2026-10-02), each rung $1$ plus one
structure, not cumulative (\code{recipes/transfer\_star\_<arm>.yaml}), pretrained at $64$k steps on hp47 and hp73, the
pretraining protocol of 2026-10-02 to 10-04 (hp73 alone since, Fig.~\ref{fig:pretrain_hp}); each arm also holds the
start-up trials that had run before the arms were cut to it (hp15, hp30, hp95) and a few leftover guided ones
(\code{tp3\_star\_<arm>}, $6$--$7.7$\,h a trial on lxplus's V100), then
fine-tuned with the grid's protocol on the arm's probe and on $ee\to d\bar d$ (\code{tp3\_s<arm>fte}; all cells in,
\code{analysis/transfer/star\_arms.py}). Four arms test the structure they name: soft and collinear emission
($+ee\to u\bar ug$, $u\bar ugg$; probe $ee\to d\bar dg$, the same topology with $d$ for $u$); initial-state collinear
($+u\bar d\to Wg$; probe $u\bar u\to Zg$); the $t\bar t$ threshold ($+u\bar u\to t\bar t$ tree and one loop at
$\sqrt s=363$--$450$; probe $ee\to t\bar t$ at one loop there: the Coulomb $1/\beta$ is a QCD one-loop term, so the
QCD-virtual target carries it); the massive quasi-collinear limit ($+ee\to b\bar bg$, $b\bar bgg$; probe $ee\to t\bar tg$,
$c$ being massless in the generator), weakly: $m_b/\sqrt s\le0.2$ against a strongly massive top. Two do not.
\emph{Narrow resonances} ($+ee\to ZZ$, $ZH$; probe $ee\to\nu_e\bar\nu_e$): the probe's resonance, the $s$-channel $Z$
pole, is already in rung $1$ ($ee\to\nu_\mu\bar\nu_\mu$, only the generation feature tells the two neutrinos apart), what
the probe adds is the $t$-channel $W$, and $ZZ$, $ZH$ (both also in rung $5$) put on-shell bosons in the final state,
which the probe has none of. \emph{Sudakov logarithms} ($+u\bar u\to d\bar d$, $ZZ$ at one loop, $\sqrt s\ge500$, both
rung-$9$ processes; probe $ee\to d\bar d$ at one loop there): every target is QCD-virtual at $\mu=\sqrt s$, so none
has the electroweak $\log^2(s/m_W^2)$; the probe is the QCD vertex correction to $\gamma^*/Z\to d\bar d$, which for
massless quarks at $\mu=\sqrt s$ is the Born times a constant (MadLoop, \code{tools/ew\_sudakov\_check.py}: finite over
Born $=0.0468$ at every point from $100$\,GeV to $3$\,TeV and three angles, constant to $10^{-16}$; so is the ladder's
one-loop probe $ee\to d\bar d$, the same target over $25$--$1000$\,GeV: its pool is the tree pool times a constant),
and the arm's processes carry boxes and initial-state vertices it lacks. Both arms' results stand as controls (unrelated
structure added to rung $1$), not as tests of their names. 

\paragraph{The redesign: the $W$ pole, and electroweak one-loop targets that carry the Sudakov logarithm.} The user's
go-ahead, 2026-10-04: a resonance arm on the $W$ pole, which no
rung before $6$ has (\code{transfer\_star\_wpole.yaml}: rung $1$ $+u\bar d\to e^+\nu_eg$, $+u\bar d\to\tau^+\nu_\tau$, a new
generator entry, $\tau$ massive; probe $u\bar d\to e^+\nu_e$, \code{udbar\_enu}, in no equality class), and a Sudakov arm
on electroweak one-loop targets (\code{transfer\_star\_sudakovew.yaml}: rung $1$ $+ee\to\mu\mu$, $u\bar u$ at one loop,
electroweak, $\sqrt s=500$--$1000$; probe $ee\to d\bar d$ there, \code{ee\_dd\_ew\_nlo}). The one-loop pipeline now
generates electroweak virtuals (\code{[virt=QED]}, \code{loop\_qcd\_qed\_sm}; the target is the finite part over
$\alpha/2\pi$ at $\mu^2=s$; the per-event guard and the certification check the universal QED double pole
$-\sum_iQ_i^2$ over the massless charged legs, met to $10^{-13}$ on all three). They carry the logarithm: for
$ee\to d\bar d$ at $90^\circ$ the finite part over the Born goes from $-6\%$ at $200$\,GeV to $-29\%$ at $3$\,TeV, a
fit in $L=\ln(s/m_W^2)$ giving $-3.7\times10^{-3}L^2$ ($-2.8$, $-4.7\times10^{-3}$ at $\cos\theta=\pm0.5$;
\code{ew\_sudakov\_check.json}); the pools change sign (finite part negative on $65$--$92\%$ of the events).

\subsection{Hand-off: open items}\label{sec:ladder-open}
\begin{itemize}
  \item \textbf{Plan to finish (the user's goal, 2026-10-05; work through it in order without stopping to ask, every
    compute item approved).} (1) The $32$k grid: scratch and every pretraining (rungs $0$--$9$) on the twelve probes at
    $D=10^{3.5}$, $10^4$, and $ud\to ud$ also at $10^{2.5}$, $10^3$, two chosen points each (\code{horizon32k\_chosen.json});
    $420$ trials, spread over lxplus, CC-IN2P3 and the $17$ done on Jean Zay (the rest moved off Jean Zay's priority queue on
    2026-10-05); waiter: a background count over the $210$ sweeps on all sites. When in: \code{cells.USE\_32K} on, figures and
    the paper draft redrawn. (2) $D=10^{4.5}$, $10^5$ at $32$k, scratch and every pretraining, two chosen points (the
    one-loop probes stop at $10^{4.5}$), drawn in the grid figures. (3) Equal-total-data rungs: rungs $2$--$9$ at $3\times10^5$
    training events in total, split evenly over the rung's processes, $64$k steps at the protocol point, and their full
    fine-tune grid (same cells and horizons as the ladder, $32$k at the top two $D$), compared with the ladder. (4) The
    finale: rung $9$ plus every star arm's processes, fine-tuned on all $18$ probes. (5) Two extra seeds at every fine-tune
    cell's best point. (6) The synthetic-amplitude generator (Sec.~\ref{sec:prior-open}, item 6), validated on CPU, then its
    pretraining fine-tuned on the twelve probes, after (3). Also: the zero-shot distances (\code{tools/zero\_shot\_ft.py},
    one job per site submitted 2026-10-05), the probes that do not gain. Throughout: placement and rebalancing across
    sites without asking; fresh collection from all sites before any figure; after every collection, and at least every
    few hours while results land, the study's own figures (\code{rung\_focus}, \code{rung\_grid}, \code{star\_arms},
    \code{gain\_heatmap}, and each new comparison) sent to the user unprompted, so a problem can be caught early;
    \code{docs/results.tex} and the paper draft (scratchpad \code{draft/}, sent as PDF and zip) kept current; reviewers run
    when due; never narrow the scope (CLAUDE.md 0b). Context guard (\code{.claude/hooks/context\_guard.py}): at $57\%$ of the
    window the notes are flushed once, at $60\%$ Claude Code compacts.
    \emph{State at the last flush (2026-10-05, 22:30):} (1) $326$ of the $420$ $32$k trials done; the $94$ missing points
    ($52$ sweeps: rung $2$ and $4$ on lxplus, rung $6$ and $7$ on CC-IN2P3) were resubmitted after the parent-copy failure

    \code{rung\_focus}, \code{rung\_grid}, \code{gain\_heatmap}, update the paper draft's numbers and send the figures.
    Decided: scratch $ee\to\nu_e\bar\nu_e$ keeps $8$k at $10^{3.5}$, takes its $32$k search (all trials) at $10^4$
    (\code{cells.HOLD\_8K}, \code{ALL\_TRIALS32}); $ud\to ud$ at $10^{2.5}$, $10^3$ goes to $32$k for every pretraining
    ($32$k lower than $8$k: scratch $3.3$, $7.8\times$, rung $7$ $1.4$, $4.7\times$). Zero-shot distances: one job per site
    (\code{FA-lxplus-42c4f7c6}, \code{FA-ccin2p3-6f199960}, \code{FA-jeanzay-4733f9ce}), lines \code{ZERO\_SHOT} in their
    logs, not yet read. Rebalancer: the new version (pending trials of any sweep) hourly over \code{tp3\_}. Next: step (2),
    the $D=10^{4.5}$, $10^5$ configs at $32$k (\code{gen\_transfer\_pilot.py} has $k=9,10$ at $16$k: write them at
    $32$k, batch $16384$, the chosen points), then (3)--(6). The paper draft is the scratchpad's \code{draft/}
    (\code{fm\_amplitudes\_updated.zip}, \code{fm\_amplitudes\_check.pdf} last sent).
  \item \textbf{$ee\to W^+W^-$.} The corner is the $|t|$ floor plus a pool that barely reaches it.
    Candidate fixes: the floor at the pool's minimum $|t|$ (regulated by $m_W$ here), and candidates
    drawn log-uniform in the initial--final invariants $|t_{ij}|$, the forward-peak analogue of the
    IR-democratic splitter, so the flat-in-$\log|\mathcal M|^2$ selection has events there to keep.
  \item \textbf{Steering, next.} A milder selection ($\gamma<3$, or a floor of mixture events) could later replace the
    mixture pool at $D\sim10^2$.
  \item \textbf{One-loop sign decision.} The choice between log and log-magnitude with a sign head
    reads the validation and test amplitudes as well as the $D$ training events; with
    \code{phase\_space\_on\_pool} it should read the training events only.
  \item \textbf{Seeds (low priority, the user's call).} Done for the scratch curves of seven probes; the
    fine-tune arm and the five probes the factors touch still need theirs, for the final curves (the seed mean
    at each cell's chosen HPs, with a band, not the search minimum).
  \item \textbf{Longer horizons: every pretraining at $32$k (2026-10-05, running).} The first $32$k round

  \item \textbf{Jean Zay on the A100 allocation (2026-10-02, the user's call).} \code{itg@v100} is in over-consumption

  \item \textbf{Jean Zay's inode quota.} \code{\$WORK} filled its $500$k-file quota on 2026-10-01 and $25$ trials
    failed or never started (retracted from their sweeps' states and replaced). Each run wrote an empty \code{source.zip}

    \code{scaling\_p1}, \code{scaling\_p1ext}, \code{scaling\_solo}, \code{finetune\_scaling}; $111$k files) are packed
    into one tar per study in \code{\$STORE/FA\_runs\_archive} (\code{scripts/archive\_runs.sh}, the user's call;
    restore with \code{tar -xf}), leaving $395$k files; the held jobs were released.
\end{itemize}

\section{Training throughput}\label{sec:throughput}
% ==========================================================================

The runs below are on V100~32GB (Jean-Zay \code{gpu\_p2}, account \code{itg@v100}); A100 was then
unavailable to the account (since 2026-10-02 the \code{itg@a100} allocation runs this project, with TF32 A/B'd: the
hand-off of Sec.~\ref{sec:ladder-open}), which matters below. A per-step optimization campaign attacked two
distinct regimes, each fast path proven numerically equal to the original by CPU equivalence
guards (\code{tests/test\_amp.py}, Section~0).

\begin{principle}[The step was data-bound before it was compute-bound]\label{prin:dataloader}
At \code{num\_workers=0}, profiling showed dataloading was $69\%$ ($260$\,ms) of a $380$\,ms
step: $8192$ serial \code{\_\_getitem\_\_} calls plus a collate concatenation on the main
thread with the GPU idle. Moving to \code{num\_workers=2} with pinned memory and a
\code{non\_blocking} host$\to$device copy overlaps the load under compute and takes the step
from $0.356$ to $0.128$\,s/iter ($2.78\times$), landing on the $\sim0.119$\,s compute floor.
This is now the default (\code{num\_workers: 2}); $nw{=}4$ is no better, $nw{=}6$ oversubscribes
the 8 CPUs and hangs.
\end{principle}

The compute floor was then lowered on two fronts. \emph{Vectorizing the on-GPU hot path} (
per-event mean pooling, the block-diagonal attention mask (built once per forward, not per
block), the per-process loss (a single segment-mean, not a Python loop over processes), the
$L_2/L_1$ regularizer (one fused \code{\_foreach}), and broadcasting per-particle local frames
across heads instead of materializing a per-head copy) gives a combined $\sim3.1\times$ at
$nw{=}0$ (dominated by the pooling rewrite, $3.08\times$ alone). A \emph{fused Adam}
(\code{fused\_optimizer}, default on) adds $1.34\times$; \emph{deferring host$\leftrightarrow$device
syncs} (collapsing $\sim4$ syncs/step into one fused \code{stack(\dots).tolist()}, with the NaN
guard moved onto the already-synced grad-norm) adds a further $\sim2\%$, small precisely
because, after Principle~\ref{prin:dataloader}, the step is no longer GPU-bound. Each vectorized
path is toggleable (\code{LLOCA\_*} env vars) with the original retained for A/B, and each is
\code{allclose}-verified against it.

\paragraph{The evaluation overhead at many processes.} A $1000$-step run on the
$478$-pool catalog with $5$k-event pools took $30$ minutes of wall time for $7.5$ minutes of
steps. The rest was the evaluation: every validation iterated $478$ per-process loaders whose
batch is capped at half a process's pool ($1000$ events), so a pass was $956$ forward calls
with a sync each ($32$\,s; twenty of them, $11$ minutes), and the post-training pass did the
same over three splits ($2900$ calls, $95$\,s) before $478$ per-process histogram pages
($113$\,s). Three changes (commits 43c18bf, 162393c): the multi-process validation and the
evaluation make one pass per split over the combined loader and accumulate the per-process
means by \code{index\_add\_} on the process ids, as the training loss does; the dataset
gained a vectorized batch fetch (\code{\_\_getitems\_\_}, one gather from the flat arrays per
batch instead of $16384$ item calls and a $16384$-slice \code{cat}); and
\code{plotting.per\_process\_pages} caps the histogram pages. A split's collection went from
$33$\,s to $8.5$\,s (the fetch was the cost, not the GPU: with \code{workers=0} the
per-item collate of a $16384$-event batch ran in the main process at $0.5$\,s a batch); the
training step is unchanged at $0.43$\,s, its two workers already hid the fetch. The two
validation paths were run on the same model (\code{LLOCA\_VAL=check}, both fetch modes): $475$
of $478$ per-process losses agree to $2\times10^{-4}$ or better; the three that do not
($ee\to u\bar u\nu\bar\nu$, $ee\to d\bar d\nu\bar\nu$, $ee\to\nu\bar\nu\nu\bar\nu$) sit at losses of
$30$--$850$ twenty steps into training and move by factors of three between two validations
of the same model on either path, so their outputs are numerically unstable at that stage
and rounding differences between a mixed and a single-process batch are amplified (the
same three, and only they, deviate with the combined batches cut to the loop's $1000$
events, so it is the composition of the batch, not its size, acting on near-divergent
outputs); not a property of the validation code, and worth a look as a struggler pattern
of its own. The
per-item fetch and the per-process loop stay behind \code{LLOCA\_FETCH=item} and
\code{LLOCA\_VAL=loop}. A $500$-step trial on the $5$k pools now takes about $6$ minutes of
wall time.

\begin{remark}[Carried but inert, and deliberately declined]
\code{allow\_tf32} is carried (default on) but is a \textbf{no-op on V100}; it would give
$\sim2\times$ matmul on A100 at $\sim10^{-3}$ precision, so it must be loss-A/B'd on A100 before
it is trusted (done for the transfer study on 2026-10-02: TF32 on and off agree within the run-to-run spread,
Sec.~\ref{sec:ladder-open}). Two optimizations were declined on purpose:
converting the $L_2$ penalty to decoupled \code{weight\_decay} (it is folded into $\vlnr$,
checkpoint selection, and the HPO objective, and $\lambda$ is a tuned sweep HP, so converting it
would silently break continuity with all existing sweep results), and \code{torch.compile} (the
compute is dominated by the torch-geometric framesnet and xformers attention, neither of which
compiles, so it buys heavy graph breaks for no gain; and it would prefix \code{state\_dict} keys,
breaking warm-start/fine-tune reloads). A gradient-checkpointing knob
(\code{checkpoint\_blocks}) trades $\sim$one extra forward for a large activation-memory saving
when memory-bound.
\end{remark}

% ==========================================================================
\section{The compute envelope: VRAM and step time}\label{sec:envelope}
% ==========================================================================

Because Jean-Zay bills a fixed GPU-hour budget, each job should run near the hardware ceiling.
Two quantities bound the operating point on the V100: peak VRAM (limiting batch size and width)
and step time (limiting how many updates fit in an allocation). The standard training batch is
therefore the largest that fits, $\mathrm{bs}=16384$ (at $\nh\!\le\!8$ on the 32\,GB V100);
for the many-process joint runs this is a statistical requirement as well as a throughput one:
at $448$ datasets and a per-dataset geometric-mean loss, $\mathrm{bs}=16384$ gives
$\sim\!36$ events/dataset per batch, where $\mathrm{bs}=1024$ would give only $\sim\!2$ and the
per-dataset MSEs (hence their log-mean) would be noise. With batch size $\mathrm{bs}$,
particles per event $n$, and width $\nh$ ($d=16\nh$), peak VRAM is bilinear,
\begin{equation}
  V_{\text{peak}}(\mathrm{bs},n,\nh) \approx a(\nh)\,\mathrm{bs}\,n + b(\nh)\,\mathrm{bs} + C(\nh)
  \quad[\mathrm{MB}],
  \label{eq:vram}
\end{equation}
with the activation coefficient $a\propto\nh$ and the fixed term $C\propto\nh^2$ (parameters
plus the two AdamW moment buffers, $\approx3\times$ params in float32; the EMA shadow adds one
more params-sized copy). Below $\mathrm{bs}=2048$ the widest tested model ($\nh=64$) fits for
all $n\le12$ on 16\,GB; at a practical $\mathrm{bs}=4096$ one fits $\nh=32$ for $n\le8$. Step
time is set by the MLP ($\mathrm{FLOPs}_{\text{MLP}}\propto n\,d^2$ dominates attention's
$n^2 d$ for $n\lesssim12$): it is linear in $n$ once the GPU is saturated
($\mathrm{bs}\gtrsim2048$) and grows as $t\propto\nh^{1.1}$, so throughput scales roughly as
$1/\nh$.

% ==========================================================================
\section{Methodological principles}\label{sec:principles}
% ==========================================================================

The results above impose a concrete protocol on how sweeps, HPO, and comparisons are run.

\begin{principle} For model comparisons through \S\ref{sec:bigrun}, compare on $\vlnr$, the
best \emph{non-regularized} validation loss on
log-amplitudes (Eq.~\ref{eq:vlnr}), never the regularized tracked loss, since $\lambda$ is a
tuned HP. Re-base for preprocessing before comparing two sides (Principle~\ref{prin:perproc}).
The transfer compute-scans and the divergence studies use their own, named metrics
(Def.~\ref{def:metric}); do
not compare their numbers against $\vlnr$.
\end{principle}
\begin{principle} Never re-sweep $\eta$ across width: $\mu$P transfers it. Tune once at the
smallest width and reuse for all larger widths, up to a $5\times$ cut on a width-scaling grid.
\end{principle}
\begin{principle} Centre the $\eta$ search by evaluating the measured 2D surface
$\eta^\ast(t,D)$ at your \emph{own} $(t,D)$: the inverted-$U$ $\eta_c(t)=10^{-2}\min[(t/3000)^{0.5},(t/3000)^{-0.55}]$
(Eq.~\ref{eq:invU}) lifted onto the high-$D$ row of the grid, and sweep only $\pm\tfrac12$
decade, not a flat 4-decade prior and not the $t$-only $\eta_c(t)$. Every real run sits past the
peak ($t\gg\tstar=3$k), so the centre \emph{decreases} with horizon; a large-$D$ foundation run
lives in the (high-$D$, high-$t$) corner the grid does not cover, so its centre is an
extrapolation of the high-$D$ decay ($\approx$2, 1.4, $0.9\times10^{-3}$ at $t=$30, 50, 100k for
per-process $D\!\approx\!70$k) and the sweep both centres and measures it. Do not hold $\eta$ at
the $\tstar$ peak, and do not discard $D$.
\end{principle}
\begin{principle} Narrow the secondary knobs to where optima actually land
(Table~\ref{tab:searchspace}): cap \code{regularization\_lambda} at $10^{-6}$, fix
\code{cosanneal\_eta\_min}$\approx10^{-8}$, keep \code{warmup\_frac}$\in[0.05,0.2]$, and drop the
exotic sampler variants ($\abs\rho\approx0.1$, noise). Together with the $\eta$ window this
shrinks the search volume $10$--$100\times$, so halve the trial budget at equal resolution or
explore far more densely at equal budget.
\end{principle}
\begin{principle} Finetune from a pretrain-tuned config: \code{lr\_scale}$\in[0.1,10]$ centred at
$1$ (best finetune $\eta\approx$ pretrain $\eta$), \code{layer\_decay}$\in[0.75,1.0]$; same
reg/warmup/$\eta_{\min}$ rules.
\end{principle}
\begin{principle} Seed new sweep cells from the law, not a flat prior: $\eta_c(t)$ is smooth in
$t$ and width-invariant, so a good rate at one horizon predicts its neighbours. Do \emph{not}
assume batch/data-size invariance; those axes were never cleanly isolated here.
\end{principle}
\begin{principle} Submit multiple sweeps \emph{interleaved} (round-robin), not all-at-once: DyHPO
trials only inform siblings that \code{observe} before they \code{suggest}, so a simultaneous
launch suggests against a stale surrogate (Def.~\ref{def:dyhpo}). When there are not enough
concurrent jobs for interleaving to serialize anything (a lone sweep, or a moment of abundant
free GPUs), fall back to \textbf{sequential batches}: split the trials into (by default) three
dependency-chained waves, so each wave \code{observe}s before the next \code{suggest}s and the
single-fidelity optimizer always has data to fit (Rem.~\ref{rem:dyhpo}).
\end{principle}
\begin{principle} A/B fairly and cheaply: reuse the existing baseline (the best of its own short
HPO sweep, never a single arbitrary run); first try the feature at the baseline's best HPs and
stop if it already wins; only if it does not, sweep the feature with the same HPO and compare
best-vs-best on $\vlnr$, re-basing for any preprocessing difference first.
\end{principle}
